Mutation-verified: reverting to the substring predicate fails exactly the two R3 tests; dropping the keys whitelist, the extends resolution, or the `when` check fails exactly their tests.

The shim now discards up to the terminating newline — keeping the head and a rolling 256-char tail — and answers exactly once with `-32600` carrying the id recovered from the line's top-level edges (id-first `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":N,` or SDK order `…"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":N}`; never a nested `"id"`). The Dockerfile build gate now runs it, and a new validate-stage `worker-opencode-ts` CI lane (digest-pinned `oven/bun:debian`, same image as the worker's builder stage) runs all five bun self-tests plus `tsc --noEmit` on every MR/push — pins in `opencode4lunarwing/package.json` + committed `bun.lock` (`@opencode-ai/sdk` 1.18.25 == the image's `OPENCODE_VERSION`, typescript 5.9.3, bun-types 1.4.0, @types/node 26.6.5) with `scripts/tsconfig.json` (`lib: ["esnext"]` so Bun's WebSocket type applies).
    - **DW-10:** idle events only end a turn once the turn was seen busy (part/status activity) or the prompt call settled — a stale idle replayed under the second event name can no longer skip a follow-up turn's permission replies. Self-test pins it with a mock whose turn-2 prompt cannot resolve until the v2 permission is answered.
    - **DW-11:** `parseTaskPrompt` in `lunarwing_runtime.ts` validates the frame (non-empty task_id, positive safe-integer seq, non-empty ≤16 KiB NUL-free prompt, boolean done) and the bridge enforces the per-task 64-prompt queue bound; refused frames are acked `accepted: false` with best-effort task_id/seq echo.
    - **DW-12:** the `envelope.payload as TaskPrompt` cast is gone (replaced by the guard); `tsc --noEmit` is clean under the pinned types, including the two pre-existing `new WebSocket(uri, {headers})` TS2353 sites (bridge client role, smoke_test) and smoke_test's `res.json()` unknowns.
    - **DW-13:** follow-ups are framed `FOLLOW-UP INSTRUCTION (seq=N[, FINAL]) from the orchestrator:`; the bridge dedupes redelivered seqs while admission is open. All three protocol files share the ack contract. Accumulated output is still unbounded in the bridge: the 16 MiB transport cap rejects oversized results and can lose the deliverable; it does not truncate safely.
    - **Historical verification at `3ca155d80` (not the !1552 follow-up):** `cargo check --lib --tests` clean (default features) and `--no-default-features --features postgres` clean; `cargo clippy --lib --tests -- -D warnings` clean (one merge artifact fixed: the `#[allow(clippy::result_large_err)]` on `handle_connection` came from the target and had been shadowed by the incoming side); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `check-boundaries.sh` PASSED; `check_no_panics.py --base 4e96648f0` OK; 62/62 external_worker lib tests (incl. the new DW-4 test), 81/81 `external_worker_integration` tests (6/6 control-channel, incl. the new DW-6 test), 34/34 web multi_tenant tests (incl. both can_prompt tests); bun 1.4.2 self-tests 5/5 (path_expand, opencode_result, task_prompt, executor incl. new DW-3/DW-5/DW-7/DW-10 cases, bridge); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `validate_config.py` + `test_goal1_ci.py` 15/15 pass with the new lane.

    - DONE (asks 1-2; ask 3 explicitly deferred) 2026-10-12 (Rarity, item-12, branch `sweetiebot/kimi-12-20261012-0128`): re-attempt of the closed MR !1528 (run-67 review verdict "REQUEST CHANGES"; DJ-1..DJ-6 all addressed in-tree). WI#332 remains open pending the owner's acceptance of the ask-3 deferral.
    - **DJ-1 (the blocker — authentication-fallback regression):** `gate_outlives_thread` exempts gates with `action_name == "authentication_fallback"` + `original_message: Some(..)` from the waiting-thread liveness rule in the predicate, the 60 s sweep, and boot-time `reconcile_pending_gate_state` (repairing the pre-existing restart inconsistency noted in the review): such gates are resolved by re-dispatching the original message, never by resuming the thread, and live until expiry exactly as before !1528. Tests: `auth_fallback_gate_outlives_its_terminated_thread` (survives sweep + listing filter + boot reconcile) and `engine_pending_gate_lookups_return_fallback_gates_for_dead_threads` (both HTTP-facing lookups return the gate, so `auth-token`/`auth-cancel` no longer 409 and `loadPendingGates` keeps the card).
    - **DJ-2 (replacement-gate race):** new `PendingGateStore::discard_if_request` compares the stored request id under the key reservation + index lock, and the sweep uses it — a replacement gate parked under the same key while the liveness read was in flight wins (same guarantee the expiry path's `run_if_absent` provides). Tests: two store-level pins (`test_discard_if_request_removes_only_the_matching_gate`, `test_discard_if_request_protects_a_replacement_gate`) and `dead_thread_sweep_never_discards_a_replacement_gate` on the blocking thread-load rig.
    - **DJ-3 (fail-closed resolution):** `resolve_gate` checks liveness right after `take_verified`; an approval that raced a thread termination no longer executes the gated action — the caller gets a "thread ended" reply and the gateway a `gate_resolved`/`thread_terminated` broadcast (the gate was already taken out of the store, so it is gone for good).
    - **DJ-4:** a persistence failure during a sweep discard is now warn-logged with the error AND the gate is still audited/broadcast as discarded, because the in-memory entry is already gone — no more silent unaudited removal with an orphaned prompt.
    - **DJ-5/DJ-6:** ENGINE-V2.md records the real contract (fallback exemption, request-id-checked sweep, fail-closed resolve race, one-sweep-interval visibility bound for already-delivered SSE cards); the agent_loop warn labels `conversation_scope()` as `scope`; `get_engine_pending_gate` filters liveness BEFORE newest-first selection so a dead newest gate cannot hide an older live one (`engine_pending_gate_lookup_skips_a_dead_newest_gate`).
    - **Ask 1:** `GET /api/chat/pending-gates` and the single-gate lookup drop gates whose thread vanished/left `Waiting`/changed owner; the 60 s sweep discards them from memory + persistence, warn-logs user/thread/request/gate/action ids, and broadcasts `thread_terminated` so open prompts clear. **Ask 2:** approvals on engine-routed channels that match no engine gate fall through to v1 with a warn naming user/channel/request id/scope (ids only, no args).

Same deferral shape as item 6's audio-capture deferral; pending the WI owner's acceptance.

The !1536 head `f0c3073e5` is merged onto target `c85ee5be9` (merge `2198d1735`, conflict-only goals resolution, this file). The review itself verified the fix works in every pebble spawn mode (real dash simulation); the open findings DV-1..DV-8 were wiring/tests/wording and are all addressed in-tree.
    - **DV-1 (Medium, the blocker — smoke could probe a different container store than the build and kill a good fat build):** mt-admin now passes its runtime explicitly (`CONTAINER_RT="$CONTAINER_RT" "$smoke_script"`, `05-tenant-build.sh`) and the smoke honours `${CONTAINER_RT:-}` before its podman-first autodetect (unknown values exit 2). Both image-mode legs run `--pull=never`, so the gate can never fall back to a registry pull of the short name. Fat builds now target `lunarwing-worker-pebble:candidate` and re-tag `:latest` only after the probe passes (then drop the candidate tag): a failed smoke can no longer leave the failing image as `:latest` (the review's "gate does not protect the image" sub-finding). Harness pins: runtime passthrough for both docker and podman, candidate-only probing, exactly one tag+rmi per fat build, slim builds untouched, and a failing-stub case asserting the build dies and `:latest` is never re-tagged.
    - **DV-3 (Low, ride-along):** the fat RUN block pre-creates `/root/go/bin`, so the existence-guarded `${GOPATH}/bin` restore holds from the very first task command (`go install x && x` in one command) instead of only after the directory appears.
    - **DV-2 (Low):** the `env -i` leg is now honestly described as proving *resolution*, not function (a stripped-env `cargo` cannot re-locate `rustc` for its own subprocesses; pebble never spawns that mode) — smoke PASS line and header, Dockerfile comment, PEBBLE-WORKER.md, and the WI#320 F6 note all reworded.
    - **DV-4 (Low — mutation gaps):** committed `SmokeScriptStubRuntimeTests` run the real smoke script against stub podman/docker binaries, pinning argv (`--pull=never --network=none`), runtime choice (env override beats autodetect, unknown runtime exits 2), and failure propagation (failing runtime → smoke exit 1 after exactly one leg — catches a never-failing `run_leg`). `DockerfileAnchorRunBlockTests` extract the anchors RUN block from the Dockerfile and execute it **verbatim under dash** against a stub toolchain tree at the real absolute paths: the happy path asserts the symlinks/wrapper exist (catches a never-true guard) and that leg 1 resolves cargo via the rustup proxies and go via the GOROOT restore while leg 2 resolves via `/usr/local/bin`; a sabotaged stub asserts loud failure (catches `|| true` on a leg).

    - **Security scope (MR !1535 review run 70, DU-1):** the checks stop partial forgeries (only the stamp or `thread_history` edited) and store corruption. They do not stop a full `mission.json` forge: setting `user_id` to the victim or a shared sentinel (`__shared__`, legacy `system`), `project_id` to the victim thread's project and adding the victim thread to `thread_history` passes all four checks, because every mission-side value compared comes from the forged record (PoC executed in the review). WI#322's write-path reservation is the fix; a mission id stamped on the thread row (under `engine/.runtime`) would be the unforgeable side
    - `resolve_stamped_interruption` now mirrors `record_restart_interrupted_thread`'s parity checks — `thread_type == Mission`, `project_id` match, owner match for non-shared missions — plus `mission.thread_history.contains(&thread_id)`; on mismatch the stamp is cleared with a `warn!` and no retry marker or notification is produced (for a partial forgery; see the security scope above).
    - Same scan-time parity check applied in `recover_pending_mission_retries` and `resume_recoverable_threads`, which trusted `thread_history` blindly (both already checked thread type; project/owner checks added, warn+skip on mismatch).
    - Tests: stamps naming a foreign user's thread, a non-Mission thread, and an own thread absent from `thread_history` each assert the stamp is cleared with no marker write and no notification; retry recovery over a planted foreign history thread asserts no retry arms and the foreign marker is untouched. `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib -- boot_resolution retry_recovery_skips`: 10/10 pass (includes the 6 pre-existing boot-resolution tests); `cargo clippy -p lunarwing_engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean
    - WI#322's `engine/projects/**` write-path reservation deliberately not landed here: it is labeled `2.1.7.0` (next release), appears in no item of this goals file, and the WI#338 cross-link comment (#7413) recommends landing #322 first or in the same pass, after which these checks defend against residual or legacy forged records. Until then they are partial protection only

The pre-WI#339 single-slot shape (`{"thread_id": ..., "recorded_at": ...}`) is still read — the WI#338 forgery tests write it verbatim and now double as legacy coverage — and is folded into the map on the next write.
    - **AE-4 (one bad row aborts the sweep):** `resolve_pending_shutdown_interruptions` isolates per mission (re-load failure → `warn!` + `continue`, stamp kept for next boot) and per stamp within a mission (resolution error → `warn!`, other stamps still resolve); the router's failure log for the sweep is promoted `debug!` → `warn!`.
    - **AE-5 (non-retry outcome notices owed at shutdown lost):** `process_mission_outcome_and_notify` stamps `pending_outcome_notification` (map keyed by thread id: response/is_error/recorded_at) when the notice is owed but undelivered and no retry marker exists to carry the `notified` flag (completion, MaxIterations, budget exhaustion, exhausted retry chain). New `deliver_pending_outcome_notifications` boot sweep emits the notices once the bridge consumer is wired (called from `router.rs` after the WI#276 resolution pass), with the stamp removal as the acknowledgement — the same best-effort semantics as WI#301/WI#276 (a crash between send and save repeats the notice at the next boot). `send_outcome_notification` now borrows the response (`Option<&str>`).
    - **Tests (8 new, in the mission test module):** the AE-3 two-fire scenario end-to-end (both stamps kept, both resolved — markers, notices, cleared stamps), legacy-stamp fold-on-write, AE-4 per-mission isolation via `failing_mission_loads` (corrupt row blocks only itself, next boot resolves it), AE-4 per-stamp isolation via a new `TestStore::failing_thread_loads` hook, AE-5 completion notice stamped + delivered once (no repeat on second sweep), AE-5 MaxIterations error notice, AE-5 negative (a retried interruption rides the marker's `notified` flag — no stamp), AE-5 no-channels (nothing owed, nothing stamped).
    - **Verification:** `cargo check -p lunarwing_engine --tests` clean; `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib` 728/728 pass (mission module 167/167 incl. all pre-existing WI#276/WI#301/WI#338 tests unchanged in behavior); `cargo clippy -p lunarwing_engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy --lib -- -D warnings` (covers `router.rs`) clean; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean.
    - **Merged onto item 14 (!1556, WI#337) on 2026-10-12** (run-79 review #7898 EN-X / #7897 EO-X):
      - **Kept from !1556:** the already-owned guards (marker, `Running`, superseded) and the stamp-first write order.
      - **Kept from this MR:** the per-stamp loop with warn-and-continue.
      - **Stamp age:** `recorded_at` is now read per thread from the map entry, with the legacy top-level fallback. A naive merge read only the top-level value, which marked every map stamp stale.
      - **Overlapping fires, newest wins:** only the newest stamped fire earns the retry; the superseded older fire is resolved record-only. This changes the AE-3 two-fire test above from "both retried".
      - **Rollback:** a stamp whose resolution fails is restored in memory before the mission's next stamp resolves.
      - **Admission:** armed retries are collected and admitted into `recovered_retries` after the fire guard drops.
      - **Tests:** 2 new tests (per-thread stale map entry; failed stamp kept when a sibling saves). The !1556 DB-1b test now asserts the isolated `Ok` result
      - **Verification:** `cargo test -p lunarwing_engine --lib runtime::mission` passes 177/177. Removing the rollback, or reading only the top-level `recorded_at`, fails 1 and 8 tests respectively.

    - **EM-1 (the blocker — the capture could not contain the crash line):** verified against the real pinned source (fetched tag `v1.18.25` → resolves to exactly `OPENCODE_REF` `cb7d8b2f5e44876ef98b661dc10590c915af3a9f`): `opencode serve` at v1.18.25 has NO per-command log flags; `--print-logs`/`--log-level` are root yargs options setting `OPENCODE_PRINT_LOGS`/`OPENCODE_LOG_LEVEL`; `loggers()` (packages/core/src/observability/logging.ts:68) returns `[fileLogger(), stderrLogger]` only when `OPENCODE_PRINT_LOGS === "1"`, else `[fileLogger()]` writing to `Global.Path.log/opencode.log` = `$XDG_DATA_HOME|~/.local/share/opencode/log/opencode.log`; the 500 middleware (server/routes/instance/httpapi/middleware/error.ts:30) logs the cause via `Effect.logError("failed", { ref, error, cause })` through that logger and answers with `message: "Unexpected server error…", ref: "err_<8 hex>"`. So the entrypoint tee (removed — serve invocation reverted to the pre-!1554 shape) could never see the crash line; the enrichment now reads the structured log instead. Bonus: the 500 body's `ref` (preserved by the SDK error wrapper under `cause.body.data.ref`) is extracted (`opencodeErrorRef`) and used for exact per-request correlation
    - **EM-2 (lone-surrogate frame drop):** the per-line cap is now by code points (`Array.from(line).slice(0, 400)`), so an astral character can never be split into a lone surrogate that would make the hub's serde_json reject the whole `task_result` and hang the job for the worker timeout. Pinned by a test placing an astral char exactly at code point 399 plus a well-formedness sweep of every attached line.
    - **EM-3 (unbounded, following read):** the log is opened `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, gated on `fstat().isFile()`, and read as one bounded ≤8 KiB window (`readSync` loop capped at TAIL_BYTES). A planted FIFO cannot wedge the task (nonblocking open + isFile reject; size-0 FIFO yields an empty window), a symlink to `/proc/<pid>/environ` or `/dev/zero` dies at `O_NOFOLLOW`, and a renamed-over directory fails `readSync` with EISDIR. The `isFile` gate is defense-in-depth: planting a device node needs CAP_MKNOD, which the same-uid attacker lacks (the one mutation-test survivor — unkillable without privileges — is documented in the test commit)
    - **EM-4 (cross-task/user leak):** the executor records the structured-log size at task start (`serveLogStartSize()`), and the window reads only from that offset; when the failing response carries a `ref`, only that request's `level=Error` line attaches (fail-closed: ref present but its line never lands ⇒ nothing attaches). One leading byte before the window disambiguates a mid-line cut (partial dropped) from a clean boundary (kept) — found and fixed by the new offset test itself.
    - **EM-5 (secret scrub):** only `level=Error` lines are eligible, and known secret values (bridge credential envs: `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`, `TENSORZERO_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `AGENT_AUTH_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `PASEO_TOKEN`, plus this task's injected `context.environment` values; ≥8 chars, ≤64 values) are replaced with `[redacted]` before the tail is returned into the job record.
    - **EM-6 (writability/false-capture):** with the tee gone there is no entrypoint capture to probe and no stale-file attach: `OPENCODE_SERVE_LOG` is a path override whose explicit empty value disables enrichment outright (no default-path fallback); unset resolves the xdg structured-log default. The structured log itself is serve-owned (append, no rotation) in the compose `opencode-data` volume — documented for operators.
    - **EM-7 (mutations, path freeze, entrypoint):** the path is resolved once at module load (`SERVE_LOG_PATH`), so a per-task credential grant named `OPENCODE_SERVE_LOG` cannot retarget or disable enrichment (`resolveServeLogPath` exported and unit-pinned; a subprocess pin proves a post-load env mutation has no effect). The test suite kills all four previously-surviving mutations (`.slice(-TAIL_LINES)` dropped, whole-file read, `TAIL_LINES=1000`, `TAIL_BYTES=1 MiB` — each bound now pinned independently) plus the EM-2 surrogate cap, the EM-3 O_NOFOLLOW, the EM-4 offset and ref filter, the EM-5 level filter, and the EM-7 per-call-env read (10 of 11 mutations dead; the isFile-gate drop is documented-unreachable, see EM-3).     - **HU-1 (Low — a `python3` shell function in the caller flipped the verdict fleet-wide):** `_openrc_proxy_python3` (`08-units-openrc.sh:474`) now uses `type -P python3` under the same fixed `PATH=/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` — the reviewer's one-word prototyped fix. `type -P` ignores shell functions (exported via `BASH_FUNC_python3%%` or defined through `BASH_ENV`) — the temporary `PATH=` assignment, not `type -P`, is what flushes bash's hash table before the lookup (run-125 IC-1) — so the bare word `python3` can no longer reach the absolute-path allowlist and 125 every guarded OpenRC operation. The resolver comment records why. Pin in `test-proxy-openrc-transition.sh` (per the review's prototype): a subshell defines `python3() { :; }`, clears `_OPENRC_PROXY_PYTHON3_RESOLVED`/`_OPENRC_PROXY_PYTHON3_RESOLVED_FROM` and `_OPENRC_PROXY_UNIT_SAFE_CACHE` so the resolution really re-runs, and asserts the safe unit still passes. Mutation-verified: reverting to `command -v` fails exactly that pin ("a python3 shell function in the caller flipped the safe verdict").
    - **HU-2 (Low, docs — the documented workaround re-opened HF-1):** `docs/ops/MULTITENANCY-PRODUCTION.md` drops the "(or set `LUNARWING_MT_PROXY_PYTHON3` to the existing spelling)" parenthetical and now says the override lives only in the calling process's environment and nothing persists it (`sudo` under `env_reset` strips it, cron and timer launchers start without it, the onboarder runs via sudo — run-125 IC-2 wording; the OpenRC watchdog and the health cron's own probes never run the guard), and any render without it switches the spelling back (archiving the override-rendered units — the harness pins this with the 'override render did not archive exactly the previous unit' and 'one-time refusal repair' assertions), so operators should prefer re-rendering to the canonical spelling. The "refused once" overstatement is corrected to the review's wording: units whose interpreter spelling differs from the canonical one are refused until re-rendered, with `upgrade-tenant` doing this automatically via `render_all_openrc_proxy_units` in `upgrade_tenant_locked` (`04-tenant-lifecycle.sh`; cited by function, not the drifting line anchor).
    - **HU-3 (Low, test strength — the two unpinned refusals):** both review-prototyped pins landed in `test-proxy-openrc-transition.sh`: a symlink at the real unit name (`$neg_dir/lunarwing-proxy-alice`) pointing at genuine safe bytes must be refused (`cmp` follows symlinks, so without the `! -L` guard a link to a safe unit would pass), and a correctly rendered body for an upper-case name (`_openrc_proxy_unit_body Alice 23104` at `lunarwing-proxy-Alice`) must be refused (the filename's tenant must survive `sanitize_name` unchanged).     - **HF-1 (Medium — contract depended on the caller's PATH; fleet-wide OpenRC refusals + repair ping-pong):** renderer and checker now share one PATH-independent interpreter resolver `_openrc_proxy_python3` (`ic/scripts/mt-admin-lib/08-units-openrc.sh`) — `LUNARWING_MT_PROXY_PYTHON3` when set, else the first `python3` under the fixed `/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin`, allowlisted to `[A-Za-z0-9._/+-]`, resolved once per process (memo keyed on the override value) — and one shared body renderer `_openrc_proxy_unit_body <name> <port>`. The checker extracts the port from the unit (strict prefix + `[0123456789]{1,5}` under `LC_ALL=C`, HF-6) and compares the file byte-for-byte against a fresh render with `cmp -s` — which also covers NUL/CR, the final newline and trailing lines, so the old byte-hygiene pass is gone. The PATH-flip shape now yields rc 0 with no archive growth; a unit rendered under a *different* resolution (e.g. pre-pin fleets, or after the override changes) is refused once and `render-units --all-proxies` repairs it — documented in `docs/ops/MULTITENANCY-PRODUCTION.md` per the must-do's release line. Pins in the transition harness: shadow-PATH render accepted under plain PATH with no archive growth, override render/accept/refuse-without/repair-archive-count, `&`-in-override refusal (HF-4), 6-digit registry port refusal (HF-6). Mutation-verified: reverting the resolver to caller-PATH `command -v python3` fails exactly the shadow-PATH pin.
    - **HF-2 (Medium — ~50x-per-check cost running on every OpenRC operation):** the per-check subshell storm (per-line `$(_openrc_re_escape …)` plus a template-RE cache that never escaped its process substitution) is replaced by the render-once/compare-once shape, plus a per-process verdict cache in `openrc_proxy_unit_is_safe`: only fully verified units are cached, keyed by path with the unit's dev:inode:mtime(ns):size signature plus the resolved interpreter, so any replacement/edit/resolution change forces a full re-check. The fast path runs the fork-heavy validations only on a miss (HU-4 correction: not fork-free — the `$(stat …)` key read forks on every hit, ~4.3 ms per hit against ~3.7 ms for a full check at the target). Measured on this VM: a 20-unit fleet `status_tenant` runs 700 guard checks as 20 renders + 680 stat-only hits; both a cold and a warm pass together took ~3.4 s in the harness on the author's VM (HU-4: run-121 measured 7.4-9.7 s at load 4-5 on 12 cores, so the 15 s budget has 1.5-2x headroom on a shared runner — the render-count pins are the deterministic part) (the !1628 head extrapolates to ~130 s; the run-113 measurement was 34.4 s for 5 tenants vs 1.0 s at the target). The remaining per-service rescan is the pre-existing quadratic shape the review explicitly left optional. Pin: `test-mt-admin-openrc-status.sh` HF-2 block — 20 rendered units, cold status pass renders exactly 20 expected bodies, warm pass 0, both passes under a 15 s budget. Mutation-verified: disabling the cache fails with "cold 20-unit status rendered 700 expected bodies".

Mutation-verified: replacing the `cmp -s` gate with `return 0` fails the extra-content negative; the NUL case is refused by byte equality (no separate check to drop).
    - **HF-4 (Info — patsub_replacement):** all `${body//@TOKEN@/…}` substitutions moved into `_openrc_proxy_unit_body` with **quoted** replacements, so bash 5.2+ `patsub_replacement` cannot expand `&` in a value; the interpreter allowlist rejects `&`/spaces/shell syntax up front.     - **HA-2 (Medium, the redirect-body echo chain):** implemented per the review's prototype — credential-injected exchanges replace the container's `Accept-Encoding` with `identity` (ordinary requests forward their original value), byte-scrub the response body with the same spellings as the headers, drop the stale upstream `Content-Length` when anything changed (hyper re-frames from the actual body), and answer 502 for a non-identity `Content-Encoding` (an uninspectable body is never forwarded blind). Pin: `proxy_redirect_body_echo_of_injected_credential_is_scrubbed` — real proxy, real TCP upstream serving a Go-style 302 with the echo in both `Location` and the body while the container asks for gzip; asserts the secret/encoded spelling absent from body and log, the marker present, and `Content-Length ==` scrubbed body length.     - **GN-5:** `redact_url_for_log` additionally strips username/password and the redirect-hop `debug!` line logs through it, so the doc's "log lines never carry the query" claim is now universally true.
    - **GN-6/GN-7:** the wrapper trait `http_request` delegates to an inherent `http_request_inner` and applies **one** guest-facing redaction (`redact_http_outcome_for_guest`) over the whole outcome — early validator errors that quote the injected URL (allowlist deny reasons, leak-scan previews) and the response `headers_json` (a `Location` echo handing the urlencoded secret to the guest) are both scrubbed alongside send errors. Pin: `guest_outcome_redaction_covers_validator_errors_and_response_headers` (mutation-verified in both directions).
    - **GN-9/GN-10:** `NETWORK_SECURITY.md` anchor corrected to `forward_request()`; both closed-port test helpers keep the listener bound with an accept-and-drop loop (deterministic, no TOCTOU).
    - **Port-matching consideration (WI ask — corrected rationale):** still deliberately not implemented; per run-107 the real reason port-matching is the wrong control is that proxy injection is plain-HTTP-only by design (HTTPS rides CONNECT, which never injects), so the leak channel is the echo, not the port. The prior trail's "LunarVision sidecar on 8088" example was a WASM-wrapper `url_prefix` credential, not a proxy `CredentialMapping`, and the enforced proxy already rejects loopback targets.
    - **Verification on this tree:** `cargo test --lib -- sandbox::proxy::http tools::wasm::wrapper tools::builtin::http` **111/111** (2 ignored pre-existing ssh-guest builds; was 108 at the !1607 head, +3 new pins); `cargo clippy --lib --tests -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; `cargo check --lib` clean. All cargo via `taskset -c 0-17`, vendored deps, no full debug builds
